### SRWToolkit: An Open Source Wizard of Oz Toolkit to Create Social Robotic Avatars
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04356v1)
- **Authors**: Atikkhan Faridkhan Nilgar, Kristof Van Laerhoven, Ayub Kinoti
- **Abstract**: We present SRWToolkit, an open-source Wizard of Oz toolkit designed to facilitate the rapid prototyping of social robotic avatars powered by local large language models (LLMs). Our web-based toolkit enables multimodal interaction through text input, button-activated speech, and wake-word command. The toolkit offers real-time configuration of avatar appearance, behavior, language, and voice via an intuitive control panel. In contrast to prior works that rely on cloud-based LLM services, SRWToolkit emphasizes modularity and ensures on-device functionality through local LLM inference. In our small-scale user study ($n=11$), participants created and interacted with diverse robotic roles (hospital receptionist, mathematics teacher, and driving assistant), which demonstrated positive outcomes in the toolkit's usability, trust, and user experience. The toolkit enables rapid and efficient development of robot characters customized to researchers' needs, supporting scalable research in human-robot interaction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SRWToolkit, an open-source Wizard of Oz (WoZ) toolkit designed for rapid prototyping of social robotic avatars using local large language models (LLMs). The toolkit enables multimodal interaction (text, button-activated speech, wake-word commands) and real-time configuration of avatar appearance, behavior, language, and voice.  A key feature is the emphasis on on-device functionality through local LLM inference, contrasting with prior cloud-based approaches.  A small user study [n=11] demonstrates the toolkit's usability, trust, and positive user experience in creating diverse robotic roles (e.g., hospital receptionist, teacher).

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Tool:** The paper presents a tangible, usable toolkit (SRWToolkit), which is a strong positive. The open-source nature encourages adoption and community contribution.
    * **Focus on Local LLMs:** The emphasis on local LLM inference addresses crucial concerns related to data privacy, user consent, and latency that are often associated with cloud-based solutions in WoZ setups. This is a well-articulated advantage.
    * **Multimodal Interaction:** Supporting multimodal interaction directly contributes to richer, more naturalistic human-robot interactions.
    * **User Study:** Though small, the user study offers initial evidence of usability and positive user experience. The selection of diverse roles is helpful.
    * **Well-structured & Clear:** The paper is well-written and clearly structured, explaining the architecture and implementation details adequately.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** While the combination of features (local LLMs, multimodal input, rapid prototyping) is useful, it's arguably an incremental improvement over existing WoZ toolkits and LLM wizard approaches. It's more of an engineering effort to integrate existing techniques effectively. The prior WoZ toolkits such as WOZ4U already presented with web-based modularity.
    * **Small User Study:** The user study is significantly underpowered (n=11). Results should be treated as preliminary. Generalizability is limited.
    * **Dependency on Google APIs (Speech):** While the focus is on local LLMs, the continued dependence on Google's Speech-to-Text and Text-to-Speech APIs somewhat undermines the commitment to local, private processing. The authors acknowledge this in the discussion.
    * **Evaluation Metrics:** The reliance on SUS, TIA and UEQ is standard, but the paper does not provide deeper, qualitative analysis of the interactions. How did the local LLMs actually *perform* in the different roles? What were the limitations?
    * **Limited comparison to baselines:** There's no direct comparison to alternative WoZ systems, either cloud-based or other local implementations. This makes it hard to quantify the benefits of SRWToolkit.

* **Significance:**

The paper's significance lies primarily in its practicality. SRWToolkit has the potential to facilitate HRI research, especially for researchers who lack extensive technical expertise and/or prioritize data privacy and low latency. The open-source release is a positive step toward wider adoption. However, the incremental nature of the technical contributions limits its overall impact. Further development with the migration of google speech to text api capabilities to local modules will significantly increase the contribution.
**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper provides a useful engineering contribution in the form of SRWToolkit (4 points). The emphasis on local LLM inference and multimodal interaction is valuable (1 point). The limited user study and reliance on Google APIs represent significant weaknesses (deduct 1 point). The paper's novelty is primarily in the *combination* of existing techniques rather than a breakthrough innovation, leading to the moderate score. Stronger user studies and complete local implementation would merit a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Connections between reinforcement learning with feedback,test-time scaling, and diffusion guidance: An anthology
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04372v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Jiao, Yuxin Chen, Gen Li
- **Abstract**: In this note, we reflect on several fundamental connections among widely used post-training techniques. We clarify some intimate connections and equivalences between reinforcement learning with human feedback, reinforcement learning with internal feedback, and test-time scaling (particularly soft best-of-$N$ sampling), while also illuminating intrinsic links between diffusion guidance and test-time scaling. Additionally, we introduce a resampling approach for alignment and reward-directed diffusion models, sidestepping the need for explicit reinforcement learning techniques.
- **Summary**: Okay, I have analyzed the provided text and will provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

This paper explores connections between several popular post-training techniques for large language models (LLMs) and diffusion models.  It focuses on reinforcement learning with human feedback (RLHF), reinforcement learning with internal feedback (RLIF), test-time scaling (specifically soft best-of-N sampling), and diffusion guidance. The paper aims to unify these seemingly disparate techniques by demonstrating equivalences and relationships under specific conditions. The key contributions include: 1) formulating RLHF and RLIF equivalently under certain parameter choices; 2) showing the asymptotic equivalence of soft best-of-N sampling to RLHF and RLIF under further assumptions; 3) proposing an alternative "RL-free alignment" approach using importance sampling for direct distribution alignment, sidestepping explicit RL; and 4) extending soft best-of-N sampling and the RL-free alignment approach to diffusion models for classifier-free guidance and reward-directed generation, respectively. The paper ultimately argues that these techniques are more closely related than previously recognized, and that a unified perspective can inform and improve the design of each.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper's novelty lies in drawing connections between techniques that have largely been developed independently. While the individual techniques (RLHF, RLIF, test-time scaling, diffusion guidance) are well-established, the explicit demonstration of their equivalences and interrelationships is a valuable contribution. The RL-free alignment approach, though building on importance sampling, is a potentially useful alternative to the more complex RLHF/RLIF pipelines. The extension of these concepts to diffusion models also contributes to novelty.  However, the extent of the novelty hinges on the rigor of the proofs and the extent to which these connections were already implicitly understood within the community. The assumption that the learned reward model is accurate, i.e., ro = r, is a significant simplification that limits the real-world applicability of some equivalences.

*Significance:*

The significance of this work is tied to its potential to simplify and improve LLM and diffusion model alignment and control. If the demonstrated equivalences hold in practice (particularly when the strong assumptions are relaxed), this could lead to more efficient and robust alignment strategies. The RL-free alignment method could offer a less computationally expensive alternative to RLHF/RLIF. However, the paper's significance is somewhat diminished by the highly theoretical nature of the analysis and the strong assumptions required to establish the equivalences. The experimental validation to support claims and assumptions are not available at this point, which makes it hard to comment on the significance.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper clearly articulates the connections between the different techniques.
*   **Mathematical Rigor:** The paper presents mathematical formulations and attempts to prove equivalences.
*   **Unifying Perspective:** The paper offers a valuable unifying perspective that can improve understanding of these methods.
*   **Potential for Simplification:** The RL-free alignment approach has the potential to simplify alignment.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Strong Assumptions:** The equivalences rely on strong assumptions, particularly concerning the accuracy of the reward model (ro = r) and faithfulness of reference policies, limiting their practical applicability.
*   **Theoretical Focus:** The paper is highly theoretical and lacks experimental validation to support the theoretical findings. Real-world applications with noisy reward models and imperfect reference policies may significantly alter the relationships.
*   **Incremental Novelty:** The core idea of importance sampling for adjusting distributions is not new; the novelty rests on applying it within this specific context.
*   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** The paper doesn't sufficiently address the limitations imposed by its assumptions or the challenges of implementing these techniques in practice.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging researchers to think more holistically about post-training techniques. The RL-free alignment approach may inspire alternative alignment strategies. However, the impact is likely to be limited unless the theoretical findings are validated experimentally and the assumptions are relaxed.

**Overall Assessment:**

While the paper presents a valuable unifying perspective and a potentially useful alternative approach, its strong assumptions, theoretical focus, and lack of experimental validation limit its current impact and significance. The paper demonstrates a decent level of novelty, but requires future work, and experiments to justify this score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 6/10

### Aesthetic Image Captioning with Saliency Enhanced MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04378v1)
- **Authors**: Yilin Tao, Jiashui Huang, Huaze Xu, Ling Shao
- **Abstract**: Aesthetic Image Captioning (AIC) aims to generate textual descriptions of image aesthetics, becoming a key research direction in the field of computational aesthetics. In recent years, pretrained Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly, leading to a significant increase in image aesthetics research that integrates both visual and textual modalities. However, most existing studies on image aesthetics primarily focus on predicting aesthetic ratings and have shown limited application in AIC. Existing AIC works leveraging MLLMs predominantly rely on fine-tuning methods without specifically adapting MLLMs to focus on target aesthetic content. To address this limitation, we propose the Aesthetic Saliency Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Model (ASE-MLLM), an end-to-end framework that explicitly incorporates aesthetic saliency into MLLMs. Within this framework, we introduce the Image Aesthetic Saliency Module (IASM), which efficiently and effectively extracts aesthetic saliency features from images. Additionally, we design IAS-ViT as the image encoder for MLLMs, this module fuses aesthetic saliency features with original image features via a cross-attention mechanism. To the best of our knowledge, ASE-MLLM is the first framework to integrate image aesthetic saliency into MLLMs specifically for AIC tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrated that our approach significantly outperformed traditional methods and generic MLLMs on current mainstream AIC benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ASE-MLLM (Aesthetic Saliency Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Model), a novel framework for Aesthetic Image Captioning (AIC). It addresses the limitations of existing MLLM-based AIC methods that often overlook the importance of focusing on aesthetic content. ASE-MLLM integrates aesthetic saliency explicitly into MLLMs. It includes the Image Aesthetic Saliency Module (IASM) to extract aesthetic saliency features and IAS-ViT (Image Aesthetic Saliency Vision Transformer) as the image encoder, which uses cross-attention to combine aesthetic saliency with original image features.  The authors claim that their approach, by integrating aesthetic saliency, helps MLLMs focus on regions of high aesthetic value, leading to improved AIC performance, achieving state-of-the-art results on benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach.  The core idea of explicitly incorporating aesthetic saliency into MLLMs for AIC is a valuable contribution.  While the individual components (CNNs, Transformers, attention mechanisms) are not new, the specific combination and adaptation for AIC is, and appears well-motivated. The design of IASM and IAS-ViT specifically to handle and fuse aesthetic saliency features is a key aspect of the paper's novelty. Prior work leveraging MLLMs for image aesthetics has largely focused on data-driven fine-tuning without explicit guidance towards aesthetic features.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the quality and relevance of AIC.  By guiding the MLLM to focus on salient aesthetic regions, the resulting captions are likely to be more informative and useful for applications such as photography education, image editing, and content creation. The reported state-of-the-art performance on AIC benchmarks, if robust, further strengthens the significance of the work. However, the impact hinges on the degree to which this approach translates into improvements over existing MLLMs *without* specialized training, as well as whether these improvements can be shown to be valuable to human users in downstream contexts.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Motivation:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing MLLM-based AIC methods and provides a solid justification for the proposed approach.
    * **Well-Defined Framework:** ASE-MLLM is a well-structured framework with clearly defined components (IASM, IAS-ViT).
    * **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate significant performance improvements compared to traditional methods and generic MLLMs.  The ablation studies provide evidence for the importance of both fine-tuning and the IASC component.
    * **Generalizability:** Experiments using a different base model demonstrate the generalizability of the framework.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Advance:** While the paper introduces a novel framework, some aspects build on established techniques (CNNs, transformers, cross-attention). The novelty lies in the integration and adaptation rather than completely new component inventions.
    * **Limited Discussion of Semantic Diversity:** The paper touches upon semantic diversity (annotations do not cover all aesthetic perspectives) as a challenge in the AIC results, but does not propose methods to specifically overcome this limitation.
    * **RPCD Dataset results are weak:** The lower results on RPCD dataset need deeper analysis. While authors claim it is due to language content differences, it is crucial to further show how the proposed method can be adapted to different datasets and linguistic styles.
    * **Lack of Ablation on Architectural Choices**: The paper claims they adopted 24 IAS-ViT blocks after experimental tests, but does not provide any ablation results. Thus, the reader does not know whether their choice is optimal or whether it is critical to performance.
    * **Limited qualitative Analysis**: Showing more examples of the produced captions would have been valuable. In addition, examples of the aesthetic saliency regions would further support their arguments.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in AIC by highlighting the importance of explicitly incorporating aesthetic saliency into MLLMs. The proposed ASE-MLLM framework can serve as a baseline for future research and inspire the development of other methods for integrating aesthetic knowledge into MLLMs. The generalizability of the approach across base models also offers flexibility for future adopters.

* **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** The paper presents a novel and well-validated framework for AIC, integrating aesthetic saliency into MLLMs. The experimental results are promising, with state-of-the-art performance on AIC benchmarks, but the limitations described above must be addressed for further improving performance.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SSGaussian: Semantic-Aware and Structure-Preserving 3D Style Transfer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04379v1)
- **Authors**: Jimin Xu, Bosheng Qin, Tao Jin, Zhou Zhao, Zhenhui Ye, Jun Yu, Fei Wu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in neural representations, such as Neural Radiance Fields and 3D Gaussian Splatting, have increased interest in applying style transfer to 3D scenes. While existing methods can transfer style patterns onto 3D-consistent neural representations, they struggle to effectively extract and transfer high-level style semantics from the reference style image. Additionally, the stylized results often lack structural clarity and separation, making it difficult to distinguish between different instances or objects within the 3D scene. To address these limitations, we propose a novel 3D style transfer pipeline that effectively integrates prior knowledge from pretrained 2D diffusion models. Our pipeline consists of two key stages: First, we leverage diffusion priors to generate stylized renderings of key viewpoints. Then, we transfer the stylized key views onto the 3D representation. This process incorporates two innovative designs. The first is cross-view style alignment, which inserts cross-view attention into the last upsampling block of the UNet, allowing feature interactions across multiple key views. This ensures that the diffusion model generates stylized key views that maintain both style fidelity and instance-level consistency. The second is instance-level style transfer, which effectively leverages instance-level consistency across stylized key views and transfers it onto the 3D representation. This results in a more structured, visually coherent, and artistically enriched stylization. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that our 3D style transfer pipeline significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods across a wide range of scenes, from forward-facing to challenging 360-degree environments. Visit our project page https://jm-xu.github.io/SSGaussian for immersive visualization.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel 3D style transfer pipeline called SSGaussian that effectively integrates prior knowledge from pre-trained 2D diffusion models to transfer high-level style semantics onto 3D scenes represented using 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS). The pipeline consists of two key stages: (1) consistent multi-view stylization using a diffusion model enhanced with a Cross-View Style Alignment (CVSA) module to maintain instance-level consistency across different viewpoints, and (2) 3DGS stylization using an Instance-level Style Transfer (IST) approach that leverages instance-level consistency to transfer style semantics in a semantically structured manner. The CVSA module incorporates cross-view attention into the UNet architecture, while the IST approach utilizes a group matching mechanism based on Identity Encoding parameters. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that the proposed pipeline outperforms existing methods in terms of stylization quality, multi-view consistency, and visual coherence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates a good degree of novelty. While 3D style transfer and the use of diffusion models for 3D tasks are not entirely new, the paper's specific combination of techniques and architectural designs contributes meaningfully to the field.

*   **Cross-View Style Alignment (CVSA):** The CVSA module is a novel contribution. It addresses a critical challenge in using 2D diffusion models for 3D style transfer—maintaining consistency across different viewpoints. By introducing cross-view attention into the UNet, the module allows for feature interactions across multiple key views, ensuring instance-level consistency.
*   **Instance-Level Style Transfer (IST):** The IST approach, combined with a group matching mechanism based on Identity Encoding parameters, is also innovative. This approach effectively leverages the instance-level consistency across stylized key views and transfers it onto the 3D representation in a semantically structured manner, leading to more visually coherent and artistically enriched stylizations.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its ability to generate high-quality, visually coherent, and consistent 3D style transfer results, addressing limitations of previous methods.

*   **Improved Stylization Quality:** The proposed pipeline effectively integrates diffusion priors and achieves superior stylization quality compared to existing methods. It captures both large-scale style semantics and fine-grained style patterns, such as brushstrokes.
*   **Enhanced Multi-View Consistency:** The CVSA module significantly improves multi-view consistency, a crucial aspect of 3D style transfer. By maintaining instance-level consistency across different viewpoints, the pipeline avoids visual artifacts and ensures that the stylized scene appears coherent from all angles.
*   **Practical Applicability:** The paper's results and efficient implementation indicate its potential for practical applications, such as virtual reality/augmented reality, content creation, and game development.
*   **Clear Improvement:** The extensive experiments and ablation studies provide convincing evidence that the proposed techniques are effective and that each component contributes to the overall performance.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing 3D style transfer methods.
*   The proposed pipeline is well-designed and addresses the identified limitations effectively.
*   The CVSA and IST modules are novel contributions that significantly improve stylization quality and multi-view consistency.
*   The experimental results are comprehensive and convincingly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed approach.
*   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
*   The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** Although the paper mentions efficient stylization, a more detailed analysis of the computational cost of each component (CVSA, IST) would be valuable. Furthermore, the user study should compare running time with competitors.
*   **Dependence on SAM:** The reliance on the Segment Anything Model (SAM) might pose a limitation. While SAM is powerful, its performance can vary depending on the scene and object complexity. The paper could discuss the potential impact of SAM's performance on the overall stylization results. A more robust pipeline would avoid this dependency on SAM, instead relying on more controllable or accurate methods.
*   **Limited Diversity of 3D Representations:** The study only focuses on 3DGS representation. Future works may extend SSGaussian to other representations, e.g., NeRF.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper is likely to influence the field of 3D style transfer by providing a new and effective approach that integrates diffusion priors and achieves high-quality, visually coherent, and consistent results. The CVSA and IST modules could be adopted by other researchers and applied to different 3D style transfer tasks. This is a solid contribution to the specific subfield of 3D Gaussian Splatting and style transfer, particularly because of the efficient runtimes.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty of the CVSA and IST modules, the significant improvements in stylization quality and multi-view consistency, and the potential for practical applications, I believe a score of 8 is appropriate. While there are minor weaknesses regarding computational cost analysis and reliance on a specific segmentation model, the paper's contributions are substantial and warrant a high score. The method has direct practical applications due to real-time rendering.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Denoising GER: A Noise-Robust Generative Error Correction with LLM for Speech Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04392v1)
- **Authors**: Yanyan Liu, Minqiang Xu, Yihao Chen, Liang He, Lei Fang, Sian Fang, Lin Liu
- **Abstract**: In recent years, large language models (LLM) have made significant progress in the task of generation error correction (GER) for automatic speech recognition (ASR) post-processing. However, in complex noisy environments, they still face challenges such as poor adaptability and low information utilization, resulting in limited effectiveness of GER. To address these issues, this paper proposes a noise-robust multi-modal GER framework (Denoising GER). The framework enhances the model's adaptability to different noisy scenarios through a noise-adaptive acoustic encoder and optimizes the integration of multi-modal information via a heterogeneous feature compensation dynamic fusion (HFCDF) mechanism, improving the LLM's utilization of multi-modal information. Additionally, reinforcement learning (RL) training strategies are introduced to enhance the model's predictive capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that Denoising GER significantly improves accuracy and robustness in noisy environments and exhibits good generalization abilities in unseen noise scenarios.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper. Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper "Denoising GER: A Noise-Robust Generative Error Correction with LLM for Speech Recognition" proposes a new framework to improve the robustness and accuracy of Generative Error Correction (GER) in Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) systems, particularly in noisy environments. The core innovation lies in a noise-robust multi-modal approach, incorporating a noise-adaptive acoustic encoder (NAAE) to extract high-quality speech embeddings, a heterogeneous feature compensation dynamic fusion (HFCDF) mechanism to better integrate acoustic and textual information, and a reinforcement learning (RL) based training strategy to enhance the model's predictive capabilities. The framework combines these components with a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM) to perform fine-grained error correction and improve ASR accuracy, even in challenging noisy conditions.  The paper demonstrates improved performance on synthesized and real-world noisy datasets, including LibriSpeech and CHIME-4, showcasing good generalization abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates several novel aspects.

*   **NAAE (Noise Adaptive Acoustic Encoder):** While adapter networks are a common approach to fine-tuning large models, the specific design and integration of a U-Net adapter into a frozen acoustic encoder, optimized for noise handling, adds a novel element. This allows for targeted fine-tuning of the acoustic features to reduce noise and improves acoustic modeling quality.
*   **HFCDF (Heterogeneous Feature Compensation Dynamic Fusion):** The dynamic fusion of acoustic and text embeddings, based on measuring the similarity between each modality and the target output, presents a novel approach to multimodal fusion. Rather than simple concatenation or cross-attention, the HFCDF mechanism uses vector differences for cross-modal compensation, helping balance understanding of speech and text. This dynamically adjusts the weight of each modality to focus on the information most relevant to the correction task.
*   **RL-based Training Strategy:** While RL-based training isn't entirely new in ASR, the specific implementation of a reward function based on WER (Word Error Rate) for optimizing the LLM during GER is a valuable addition. This directly aligns the training objective with the desired evaluation metric, which addresses a common mismatch in traditional cross-entropy training.

However, it is important to note some limitations.

*   While the combination is novel, the individual components are built on existing research. Adapter networks, UNet architectures, and cross-modal attention mechanisms are well-established techniques. The innovation lies in the specific combination and adaptation to the GER task in noisy environments.
*   The overall architecture follows the general paradigm of integrating LLMs with ASR systems for post-processing. While the specific implementation details are novel, the high-level concept is not groundbreaking.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a critical challenge in ASR: robustness to noise. The improved performance demonstrated on various noisy datasets suggests that the proposed framework offers a meaningful improvement over existing methods.

*   **Practical Impact:** By improving GER performance in noisy environments, the framework has the potential to enhance the usability of ASR systems in real-world applications such as voice assistants, transcription services, and speech-based interfaces.
*   **Generalization Ability:** The demonstrated performance on unseen noise scenarios and clean speech suggests good generalization capabilities, which is crucial for practical deployment.

However,

*   While the experiments are comprehensive, further evaluation on larger and more diverse datasets would strengthen the claims of robustness and generalization ability.
*   Comparisons to more recent and cutting-edge baselines (beyond MMGER) would better establish the state-of-the-art performance of the proposed framework.
*   The paper would benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity and resource requirements of the proposed framework, which are important considerations for real-world deployment.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a well-designed framework with several novel components that contribute to improved noise robustness in ASR. The experimental results demonstrate a significant improvement over existing methods. However, some limitations need to be considered.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The score reflects the paper's strong points: the novelty of the combined architecture, the effective integration of noise-adaptive components, and the demonstrated performance improvements on noisy datasets. The significance lies in the practical impact of improving ASR robustness in real-world environments.

However, the score is also tempered by the fact that the individual components are based on existing techniques and the lack of comparison to some recent and state-of-the-art ASR techniques. Also the limited discussions on computational costs reduces the overall significance. A slightly higher score could be justified with more detailed benchmarking and a deeper analysis of the framework's limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Transition Models: Rethinking the Generative Learning Objective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04394v1)
- **Authors**: Zidong Wang, Yiyuan Zhang, Xiaoyu Yue, Xiangyu Yue, Yangguang Li, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: A fundamental dilemma in generative modeling persists: iterative diffusion models achieve outstanding fidelity, but at a significant computational cost, while efficient few-step alternatives are constrained by a hard quality ceiling. This conflict between generation steps and output quality arises from restrictive training objectives that focus exclusively on either infinitesimal dynamics (PF-ODEs) or direct endpoint prediction. We address this challenge by introducing an exact, continuous-time dynamics equation that analytically defines state transitions across any finite time interval. This leads to a novel generative paradigm, Transition Models (TiM), which adapt to arbitrary-step transitions, seamlessly traversing the generative trajectory from single leaps to fine-grained refinement with more steps. Despite having only 865M parameters, TiM achieves state-of-the-art performance, surpassing leading models such as SD3.5 (8B parameters) and FLUX.1 (12B parameters) across all evaluated step counts. Importantly, unlike previous few-step generators, TiM demonstrates monotonic quality improvement as the sampling budget increases. Additionally, when employing our native-resolution strategy, TiM delivers exceptional fidelity at resolutions up to 4096x4096.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Transition Models (TiM), a novel generative learning paradigm designed to address the trade-off between fidelity and computational cost in generative modeling.  TiM is based on learning the state transitions along the Probability Flow Ordinary Differential Equation (PF-ODE) trajectory over arbitrary time intervals (Δt). This approach allows the model to adapt to different step sizes, facilitating both efficient few-step generation and high-fidelity refinement with more steps.  The authors claim TiM achieves state-of-the-art performance in text-to-image generation compared to models with significantly more parameters (SD3.5 and FLUX.1) across a range of evaluation metrics, resolutions, and aspect ratios.  Key contributions include the novel learning objective, an efficient derivative calculation method (Differential Derivation Equation - DDE), and architectural improvements to DiT.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *learning objective* itself – training a model to directly predict state transitions over *arbitrary* time intervals. This is a departure from approaches that focus either on infinitesimal dynamics (traditional diffusion) or direct endpoint prediction (consistency models).  The authors present a mathematical justification based on a "State Transition Identity," derived from the PF-ODE framework.  The architectural modifications, while beneficial, seem more like engineering optimizations than fundamental breakthroughs, especially given the use of LoRA-AdaLN with DiT. The DDE for efficient gradient estimation is a valuable practical contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance of TiM is high, *if* the claims hold up robustly under further scrutiny. The ability to train a smaller model (865M parameters) to outperform significantly larger models (8B-12B parameters) while maintaining scalability with step count would be a substantial advance. The proposed method reduces computational overhead while maintaining or even improving quality. The claims regarding generalization across various resolutions and aspect ratios also strengthen the impact, if true.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper presents compelling results on GenEval, MJHQ30K, and DPGBench, demonstrating superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods. The results are consistent across various evaluation metrics.
    *   **Principled approach:** The method is rooted in a well-defined mathematical framework, providing theoretical justification for the proposed learning objective. The derivation of the State Transition Identity provides insights into the training process.
    *   **Scalability:** The DDE enables efficient training, which is essential for scaling to large datasets and models.
    *   **Addressing a core trade-off:** Directly tackles the long-standing trade-off between speed and quality in generative modeling.
    *   **Clear ablation studies:** The ablation studies provide a solid understanding of the contributions of different components (loss weighting, architecture improvements, etc.).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited architectural innovation:** The model architecture relies heavily on existing components (DiT and variations) with only moderate improvements.
    *   **Dependence on PF-ODE:** Is TiM effective for other SDEs? The PF-ODE framework restricts the exploration scope and the paper might need to address this limitation.
    *   **Dependence on Data:** It was observed that the underlying autoencoder might limit resolution in some scenarios.

*   **Justification for Score:** TiM proposes a novel training objective that leads to significant improvements in generative modelling tasks. Additionally, it provides comprehensive experimental results. However, its reliance on existing architectures and the PF-ODE framework hinder the significance of the work. 

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Self-adaptive Dataset Construction for Real-World Multimodal Safety Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04403v1)
- **Authors**: Jingen Qu, Lijun Li, Bo Zhang, Yichen Yan, Jing Shao
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are rapidly evolving, presenting increasingly complex safety challenges. However, current dataset construction methods, which are risk-oriented, fail to cover the growing complexity of real-world multimodal safety scenarios (RMS). And due to the lack of a unified evaluation metric, their overall effectiveness remains unproven. This paper introduces a novel image-oriented self-adaptive dataset construction method for RMS, which starts with images and end constructing paired text and guidance responses. Using the image-oriented method, we automatically generate an RMS dataset comprising 35k image-text pairs with guidance responses. Additionally, we introduce a standardized safety dataset evaluation metric: fine-tuning a safety judge model and evaluating its capabilities on other safety datasets.Extensive experiments on various tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed image-oriented pipeline. The results confirm the scalability and effectiveness of the image-oriented approach, offering a new perspective for the construction of real-world multimodal safety datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Self-adaptive Dataset Construction for Real-World Multimodal Safety Scenarios":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing concern of safety in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It argues that existing safety datasets are inadequate because they rely on risk-oriented, often synthetic data, failing to capture the complexities of real-world multimodal safety scenarios (RMS). To address this, the paper introduces a novel image-oriented self-adaptive dataset construction method. The approach begins with real-world images and systematically generates paired text and guidance responses, leveraging information complementarity (where individually safe modalities combine to create unsafe scenarios). The paper introduces a Real-World Multimodal Safety Scenarios (RMS) dataset of 35k image-text pairs.  Furthermore, the paper introduces a standardized evaluation metric for safety datasets by fine-tuning a safety judge model and evaluating its performance on other datasets.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed image-oriented pipeline in identifying RMS.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of an image-oriented, self-adaptive approach to dataset construction for MLLM safety is novel.  Instead of *starting* with predefined risks and generating content around them (a common approach), the paper starts with real-world images and *discovers* risks through the principle of information complementarity. Also, the RMS dataset adds significant value with the emphasis on real-world situations versus synthetic/simulation data.  Finally, the proposed metric for evaluating safety dataset quality via fine-tuning a "safety judge" is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of current MLLM safety research, which often overlooks the complexities of real-world scenarios. A dataset built using real-world images and addressing information complementarity offers a more robust and generalizable testbed for improving MLLM safety. Also, it provides a new perspective for dataset construction. The standardized evaluation metric could significantly advance the field by enabling better comparisons between different safety datasets and alignment techniques.  The work's impact is amplified by the public availability of the RMS dataset.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Image-oriented Approach:** The shift from risk-oriented to image-oriented construction is a significant advantage, promoting more realistic and diverse safety scenarios.
    *   **Information Complementarity:** Exploiting this principle allows the construction of challenging scenarios where risks are not obvious from individual modalities.
    *   **Automated Pipeline:**  The automated pipeline increases scalability and reduces the need for manual annotation.
    *   **Standardized Evaluation Metric:** This addresses a significant gap in the field and enables better comparison and evaluation of safety datasets.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes a variety of experiments that demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed method and dataset.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Inspiration Dataset:** The effectiveness of the method is contingent on the quality and diversity of the initial image dataset (COCO in this case). Although COCO provides good real-world image data, this still limits the possible scenarios and risks.
    *   **Potential for Bias:** While the paper mentions efforts to mitigate bias, the automated nature of the pipeline raises concerns about potential biases present in the training data being amplified. The safety review procedure and manual checks help, but aren't guaranteed to eliminate this risk.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** While experiments are thorough, a deeper dive into the *types* of failures observed on the RMS dataset, compared to other datasets, would strengthen the analysis. More ablation studies on different components of the pipeline are welcome, too.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of MLLM safety research.  It offers a more realistic and scalable approach to dataset construction, which can ultimately lead to safer and more robust MLLMs.  The standardized evaluation metric could also promote the development of more effective safety alignment techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 8.  The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of MLLM safety. The image-oriented dataset construction method and the standardized evaluation metric are both valuable advancements. The RMS dataset fills a critical gap by providing a more realistic and challenging testbed for MLLM safety research. The experiments provide solid evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The weaknesses related to data bias and limited evaluation are valid points but are reasonably addressed within the context of the paper and do not substantially diminish its overall contribution. Also, this is a newly explored topic, and improvements are possible with the help of further contributions.

### Few-step Flow for 3D Generation via Marginal-Data Transport Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04406v1)
- **Authors**: Zanwei Zhou, Taoran Yi, Jiemin Fang, Chen Yang, Lingxi Xie, Xinggang Wang, Wei Shen, Qi Tian
- **Abstract**: Flow-based 3D generation models typically require dozens of sampling steps during inference. Though few-step distillation methods, particularly Consistency Models (CMs), have achieved substantial advancements in accelerating 2D diffusion models, they remain under-explored for more complex 3D generation tasks. In this study, we propose a novel framework, MDT-dist, for few-step 3D flow distillation. Our approach is built upon a primary objective: distilling the pretrained model to learn the Marginal-Data Transport. Directly learning this objective needs to integrate the velocity fields, while this integral is intractable to be implemented. Therefore, we propose two optimizable objectives, Velocity Matching (VM) and Velocity Distillation (VD), to equivalently convert the optimization target from the transport level to the velocity and the distribution level respectively. Velocity Matching (VM) learns to stably match the velocity fields between the student and the teacher, but inevitably provides biased gradient estimates. Velocity Distillation (VD) further enhances the optimization process by leveraging the learned velocity fields to perform probability density distillation. When evaluated on the pioneer 3D generation framework TRELLIS, our method reduces sampling steps of each flow transformer from 25 to 1 or 2, achieving 0.68s (1 step x 2) and 0.94s (2 steps x 2) latency with 9.0x and 6.5x speedup on A800, while preserving high visual and geometric fidelity. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms existing CM distillation methods, and enables TRELLIS to achieve superior performance in few-step 3D generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MDT-dist, a novel framework for accelerating flow-based 3D generation models through few-step distillation. Traditional flow-based models require many iterative sampling steps, hindering their practicality. MDT-dist aims to address this by distilling a pre-trained model into a faster generator. The core idea is to learn the marginal-data transport, mapping any marginal distribution to the data distribution.  Since directly learning this is difficult, the paper proposes two optimizable objectives: Velocity Matching (VM) and Velocity Distillation (VD). VM matches velocity fields between student and teacher models, while VD leverages these fields to perform probability density distillation. The method is evaluated on TRELLIS, a state-of-the-art 3D generation framework, demonstrating significant speedup with preserved visual and geometric fidelity compared to existing Consistency Model (CM) distillation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its approach to few-step 3D flow distillation. While consistency models have been used to accelerate 2D diffusion models, their application to 3D is less explored. The MDT-dist framework, particularly the VM and VD objectives, offers a distinct approach compared to existing distillation methods. Deriving objectives directly from the marginal data transport objective is also a key novelty, overcoming some limitations of CMs. The approach of using *both* velocity matching and velocity distillation is another novel aspect, addressing the limitations of each approach individually.
*   **Significance:** Accelerating 3D generation is crucial for making it more practical for applications in embodied AI and interactive graphics. MDT-dist's ability to significantly reduce sampling steps (from 25 to 1-2) while maintaining quality is a significant step towards this goal. The performance gains demonstrated on TRELLIS, a high-quality 3D generation model, highlight the potential impact of the method. The improved visual and geometric quality compared to FlashVDM is also a valuable contribution. The paper is also significant as it introduces a novel framework that can be applied to a wide range of 3D flow-based models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem of slow inference in flow-based 3D generation.
    *   It provides a well-motivated approach based on marginal-data transport and addresses the difficulties in directly learning it by defining Velocity Matching and Velocity Distillation objectives.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate significant speedups and maintained or improved quality compared to existing methods, particularly over CM based distillation methods like PCM (Phased Consistency Models).
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component (VM and VD).
    *   The appendix provides a more detailed proof on demonstrating error bound of the primary objective and rationale for replacing the -gradient for score distillation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the results are promising, the method might be highly dependent on the quality of the pre-trained teacher model. The paper acknowledges that a possible improvement is to eliminate dependence of geometrical data input which would reduce the costs.
    *   The method currently operates in a latent space, potentially limiting its applicability to models that do not use such latent spaces.
    *   Although the comparison to FlashVDM highlights improvements, the evaluation primarily focuses on TRELLIS, with more exploration of performance across different 3D flow generation architectures remaining to be done.
*   **Potential Influence:**  MDT-dist has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical solution for accelerating 3D generation. It opens doors for further research on distillation techniques tailored for 3D flow models, particularly addressing the challenges posed by the discrete and sparsely structured nature of 3D data. Future work might explore the theoretical properties of the VM and VD objectives in more depth, as well as its potential usage with generative adversarial networks.

**Rationale for Score:**

I assign a score of 7 to this paper. The approach is well-motivated, technically sound, and shows significant practical improvements. The limitations, while present, do not overshadow the value of the contribution. The paper provides a novel framework with strong experimental evidence, suggesting a promising direction for future research in few-shot 3D generation distillation. The significance of achieving substantial speedups while maintaining quality is noteworthy. The main drawbacks lie on its reliance on teacher model and latent space requirement.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Durian: Dual Reference-guided Portrait Animation with Attribute Transfer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04434v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunsoo Cha, Byungjun Kim, Hanbyul Joo
- **Abstract**: We present Durian, the first method for generating portrait animation videos with facial attribute transfer from a given reference image to a target portrait in a zero-shot manner. To enable high-fidelity and spatially consistent attribute transfer across frames, we introduce dual reference networks that inject spatial features from both the portrait and attribute images into the denoising process of a diffusion model. We train the model using a self-reconstruction formulation, where two frames are sampled from the same portrait video: one is treated as the attribute reference and the other as the target portrait, and the remaining frames are reconstructed conditioned on these inputs and their corresponding masks. To support the transfer of attributes with varying spatial extent, we propose a mask expansion strategy using keypoint-conditioned image generation for training. In addition, we further augment the attribute and portrait images with spatial and appearance-level transformations to improve robustness to positional misalignment between them. These strategies allow the model to effectively generalize across diverse attributes and in-the-wild reference combinations, despite being trained without explicit triplet supervision. Durian achieves state-of-the-art performance on portrait animation with attribute transfer, and notably, its dual reference design enables multi-attribute composition in a single generation pass without additional training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Durian paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Durian, a novel method for generating portrait animation videos that allows for attribute transfer from a reference image to a target portrait in a zero-shot manner. The core innovation lies in its "dual reference" network architecture. This architecture injects spatial features from both the attribute image and the target portrait image into the denoising process of a diffusion model. The model is trained using a self-reconstruction formulation, effectively learning to transfer attributes without explicit triplet supervision. Additional techniques such as mask expansion (to handle varying attribute sizes) and spatial/appearance augmentations (to improve robustness to misalignment) are employed to further improve performance. A significant claim is that Durian enables multi-attribute composition within a single pass, a feat not easily achievable by prior methods without retraining.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to portrait animation with attribute transfer. The dual-reference network and self-reconstruction training framework is a meaningful departure from traditional methods that rely on triplet data or explicit mask annotations. The techniques for spatial mask expansion and augmentation contribute to a more robust and generalizable model. The zero-shot capability for unseen identities and multi-attribute transfer are key strengths. While existing methods handle single attribute image editing and animation separately, this method integrates attribute transfer and animation, which to the best of my knowledge, has not been done before.

*   **Significance:** The approach has the potential to significantly impact the fields of image and video editing, as well as AR/VR applications. The zero-shot capabilities make it highly adaptable to various attributes and user scenarios. If Durian indeed delivers on the promises of high fidelity, attribute consistency, and seamless animation in a single forward pass, it simplifies the editing workflow compared to multistage pipelines. The code has not been released so the results are to be taken with a grain of salt.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dual Reference Network:** The core innovation of the dual reference network architecture allows the diffusion model to accurately transfer the attributes from a reference image onto the portrait.
    *   **Zero-Shot Capabilities:** Achieves convincing results on unseen identities and diverse attributes without requiring specific fine-tuning, increasing scalability.
    *   **Multi-Attribute Composition:** Handles multiple attribute transfer in a single pass, eliminating the need for sequential editing or retraining.
    *   **Robustness and Generalization:** The introduction of spatial mask expansion and tailored augmentation improve robustness to pose and attribute variations.
    *   **Self-Reconstruction Training Formulation:** This avoids the costly need for triplet data generation.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Keypoint Extraction:** The method relies on accurate facial keypoint detection and tracking. Failures in this front-end could lead to artifacts or temporal jitter in the generated animation.
    *   **Limitations with Complex Interactions:** Complex attribute interactions (e.g., hair occluded by hats) might not be handled perfectly.
    *   **Sensitivity to Lighting Differences:** Large lighting variations between the portrait and reference images might still degrade the visual quality, although the augmentation tries to minimize this.
    *   **Limited Pose Variations:** The model is primarily trained on front-facing portraits, potentially limiting performance on extreme poses or back views. This could pose an issue in real world conditions.
    *   **Evaluation Metric Limitations:** The self-attribute transfer evaluation might not fully capture the complexity of true cross-identity attribute transfer.

*   **Impact and Influence:** The zero-shot cross-identity attribute transfer with smooth animation is a valuable contribution. As diffusion models become more prevalent, efficient and controllable mechanisms like those proposed by Durian will be highly sought after. It could inspire future research in more robust and controllable video generation techniques.

*   **Justification of Score:** The work presents a good level of novelty and has significant potential impact. While the reliance on keypoint extraction and the limitations regarding complex scenes/extreme poses are legitimate concerns, the core ideas and promising results justify a relatively high score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Delta Activations: A Representation for Finetuned Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04442v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiqiu Xu, Amish Sethi, Mayur Naik, Ser-Nam Lim
- **Abstract**: The success of powerful open source Large Language Models (LLMs) has enabled the community to create a vast collection of post-trained models adapted to specific tasks and domains. However, navigating and understanding these models remains challenging due to inconsistent metadata and unstructured repositories. We introduce Delta Activations, a method to represent finetuned models as vector embeddings by measuring shifts in their internal activations relative to a base model. This representation allows for effective clustering by domain and task, revealing structure in the model landscape. Delta Activations also demonstrate desirable properties: it is robust across finetuning settings and exhibits an additive property when finetuning datasets are mixed. In addition, we show that Delta Activations can embed tasks via few-shot finetuning, and further explore its use for model selection and merging. We hope Delta Activations can facilitate the practice of reusing publicly available models. Code is available at https://github.com/OscarXZQ/delta_activations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Delta Activations: A Representation for Finetuned Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Delta Activations," a method to represent fine-tuned language models (LLMs) as vector embeddings by measuring shifts in their internal activations relative to a base model.  The authors argue that this representation addresses the challenge of navigating and understanding the growing collection of post-trained models, which often lack consistent metadata and are stored in unstructured repositories.  Delta Activations are shown to enable effective clustering by domain and task, be robust across finetuning settings, exhibit an additive property when mixing finetuning datasets, embed tasks via few-shot finetuning, and be useful for model selection and merging.  The authors provide experimental results demonstrating these properties.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using the difference in internal activations between a base model and a finetuned model as an embedding is reasonably novel, though the general concept of creating model embeddings is not. Several previous works already try to create model embeddings using adapter weights, or training-data-dependent features. The key innovation is the approach to extract an embedding from measuring the shift in internal activations. The authors did a good job by comparing the difference between Delta Activation with those methods. A clear strength is that the representation is self-contained (derived only from the model itself) and doesn't require external data. However, some prior work has already used activation shifts to transfer/edit behavior across models (cited by the authors). Thus, novelty resides in the specific *application* and validation of this concept as a general-purpose representation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem: managing the proliferation of finetuned LLMs.  A method for easily comparing, clustering, and selecting models would be valuable for the community. The experiments provide evidence that Delta Activations have promise in these areas. The findings that the representation is robust to variations in training and exhibits additive properties are significant, suggesting that the embeddings capture core aspects of the model's behavior. The "Delta-X" generalization opens the door to further exploration of different representations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clearly Defined Method:** The paper clearly explains the Delta Activations approach.
    *   **Strong Experimental Evaluation:**  The experiments are well-designed and cover a range of scenarios: clustering by domain, studying robustness to training settings, few-shot task embedding, cross-base model clustering, and model selection. The use of multiple backbones (LLaMA, Gemma, Qwen) strengthens the claims.
    *   **Addresses a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a real challenge in the LLM community.
    *   **"Delta-X" Framework:** The generalization to other features beyond activations expands the potential impact.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments primarily focus on relatively simple domain classification tasks.  It's not clear how well Delta Activations would generalize to more complex tasks or evaluations that require nuanced understanding of model capabilities (e.g., bias detection, safety analysis).
    *   **Requires Access to Internal States:** This could be a significant limitation in practice, especially with proprietary models. The paper only addresses this indirectly with the Delta Meaning extension, which is evaluated more limitedly.
    *   **Model Merging Experiment is Weak:** The model merging experiment is a starting point but only uses a simple nearest-neighbor approach, and the results are mixed.
    *   **Lack of ablation study:** The authors mentioned that the probe dataset should be completely generic. However, there is no ablation study to explain *why* generic prompts are better than task-specific prompts.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the method proves scalable and generalizable, it could become a standard tool for model management and reuse. The Delta-X framework could inspire further research into alternative model representations. The focus on internal states is valuable.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a reasonable solution to address the challenge in managing and reusing fine-tuned LLMs.  It presents a simple yet effective representation based on internal activation shifts and validates its properties through well-designed experiments. The framework also provide the potential to further improvements.

The limitations are access to internal activation and limited scope of evaluation.

Therefore, the paper merits a score of 7.5.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Plot'n Polish: Zero-shot Story Visualization and Disentangled Editing with Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04446v1)
- **Authors**: Kiymet Akdemir, Jing Shi, Kushal Kafle, Brian Price, Pinar Yanardag
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image diffusion models have demonstrated significant capabilities to generate diverse and detailed visuals in various domains, and story visualization is emerging as a particularly promising application. However, as their use in real-world creative domains increases, the need for providing enhanced control, refinement, and the ability to modify images post-generation in a consistent manner becomes an important challenge. Existing methods often lack the flexibility to apply fine or coarse edits while maintaining visual and narrative consistency across multiple frames, preventing creators from seamlessly crafting and refining their visual stories. To address these challenges, we introduce Plot'n Polish, a zero-shot framework that enables consistent story generation and provides fine-grained control over story visualizations at various levels of detail.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "Plot'n Polish: Zero-shot Story Visualization and Disentangled Editing with Text-to-Image Diffusion Models" addresses a prominent challenge in the application of text-to-image diffusion models for creative storytelling. It highlights the limitations of existing methods that struggle to provide flexible editing options while maintaining both visual and narrative consistency across multiple frames in generated story visuals. To tackle these challenges, the authors introduce a novel framework called Plot'n Polish, designed for zero-shot story generation. This framework enables users to exert fine-grained control over story visualizations, allowing for both detailed adjustments and larger edits without compromising the coherence of the story being told. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty:** The concept of applying text-to-image diffusion models for storytelling is itself not entirely new, as various prior works have explored this domain. However, the innovative aspect of Plot'n Polish lies in its focus on zero-shot generation and specific disentangled editing capabilities. The ability to generate a series of visuals that are both coherent narratively and visually consistent, without requiring retraining or extensive pre-setup, adds a unique angle to the existing body of work. However, it remains to be evaluated how these improvements stack up against state-of-the-art methods. **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial, as it addresses real-world challenges faced by creators in the burgeoning field of digital story visualization. By providing tools that enhance control and consistency, it has the potential to greatly influence artists, writers, and content creators, effectively democratizing access to sophisticated story visualization techniques. Furthermore, the zero-shot aspect enhances usability, broadening the framework's appeal beyond specialists. However, the paper could be stronger in articulating the practical implications and providing case studies or user evaluations to substantiate its claims. **Strengths:** - Introduces a specific framework tackling a clear problem in story visualization. - Focuses on user-oriented control and editing capabilities. - Advances the understanding of how diffusion models can be adapted for creative applications. **Weaknesses:** - Limited comparative evaluation against existing methods may leave readers questioning the framework's relative effectiveness. - Lack of detailed user-testing results could hinder understanding of real-world applicability. - The application scope remains somewhat ambiguous in terms of variety and complexity of stories that can be handled. **Potential Influence:** This work could serve as a foundational step for future research that further develops tools for storytellers. Additionally, its approach to editing and control may inform subsequent innovations in AI-driven creative tasks beyond storytelling, such as game and scenario design. **Score:** 7 The score reflects the paper's solid contribution and innovative ideas while acknowledging the limitations in experimental validation and comparative analysis. However, its potential impact in enhancing creative story visualization remains promising, justifying a commendable score of 7.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
